					<h1 id="firstHeading" class="firstHeading mw-first-heading"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Pitcairn Islands</span></h1>
				
							
<div id="p-lang-btn" class="vector-menu vector-dropdown vector-menu-dropdown mw-portlet mw-portlet-lang mw-ui-icon-flush-right"  >
	<input type="checkbox"
		id="p-lang-btn-checkbox"
		role="button"
		aria-haspopup="true"
		data-event-name="ui.dropdown-p-lang-btn"
		class="vector-menu-checkbox mw-interlanguage-selector"
		aria-label="Go to an article in another language. Available in 125 languages"
		
	/>
	<label
		id="p-lang-btn-label"
		for="p-lang-btn-checkbox"
		class="vector-menu-heading mw-ui-button mw-ui-quiet mw-ui-progressive mw-portlet-lang-heading-125"
		
	>
		<span class="mw-ui-icon mw-ui-icon-wikimedia-language-progressive"></span><span class="vector-menu-heading-label">125 languages</span>

class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Time_zone" title="Time zone">Time zone</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/UTC-08:00" class="mw-redirect" title="UTC-08:00">UTC-08:00</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Date format</th><td class="infobox-data">dd/mm/yyyy</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Left-_and_right-hand_traffic" title="Left- and right-hand traffic">Driving side</a></th><td class="infobox-data">left</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Telephone numbers in the Pitcairn Islands">Calling code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_New_Zealand" title="Telephone numbers in New Zealand">+64</a></td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Postal_codes_in_the_Pitcairn_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Postal codes in the Pitcairn Islands">UK postcode</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><div class="postal-code">PCRN 1xx</div></td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166" title="ISO 3166">ISO 3166 code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:PN" title="ISO 3166-2:PN">PN</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.pn" title=".pn">.pn</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Website</th><td class="infobox-data"><a rel="nofollow" class="external free" href="https://www.government.pn/">https://www.government.pn/</a></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1041539562">.mw-parser-output .citation{word-wrap:break-word}.mw-parser-output .citation:target{background-color:rgba(0,127,255,0.133)}</style><span class="citation wikicite" id="endnote_a"><b><a href="#ref_a">^</a></b></span>  The <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Islands_dollar" title="Pitcairn Islands dollar">Pitcairn Islands dollar</a> is also official legal tender, although it does not circulate as widely.</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The <b>Pitcairn Islands</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;p&#39; in &#39;pie&#39;">p</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/ɛər/: &#39;are&#39; in &#39;bare&#39;">ɛər</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span></span>/</a></span></span>;<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pitkern" class="mw-redirect" title="Pitkern">Pitkern</a>: <i><span title="Pitcairn-Norfolk-language text"><i lang="pih">Pitkern Ailen</i></span></i>), officially the <b>Pitcairn, Henderson, Ducie and Oeno Islands</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-BNA1981_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BNA1981-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Order2010_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Order2010-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-PitcainLaws_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcainLaws-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-TheOverTerr_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TheOverTerr-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> is a group of four volcanic islands in the southern <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a> that form the sole <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territories" title="British Overseas Territories">British Overseas Territory</a> in the Pacific Ocean. The four islands—<a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Island" title="Pitcairn Island">Pitcairn</a>, <a href="/wiki/Henderson_Island_(Pitcairn_Islands)" title="Henderson Island (Pitcairn Islands)">Henderson</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ducie_Island" title="Ducie Island">Ducie</a> and <a href="/wiki/Oeno_Island" title="Oeno Island">Oeno</a>—are scattered across several hundred miles of ocean and have a combined land area of about 18 square miles (47&#160;km<sup>2</sup>). Henderson Island accounts for 86% of the land area, but only Pitcairn Island is inhabited. The islands nearest to the Pitcairn Islands are <a href="/wiki/Mangareva" title="Mangareva">Mangareva</a> (of <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a>) at 688 km to the west and <a href="/wiki/Easter_Island" title="Easter Island">Easter Island</a> at 1,929 km to the east.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Islanders" title="Pitcairn Islanders">Pitcairn Islanders</a> are a biracial ethnic group descended mostly from nine <a href="/wiki/Mutiny_on_the_Bounty" title="Mutiny on the Bounty"><i>Bounty</i> mutineers</a> and a handful of <a href="/wiki/Tahitians" title="Tahitians">Tahitian</a> consorts—as is still apparent from the surnames of many of the islanders. The mutiny and its aftermath have been the subject of many books and films. As of January 2020, the territory had only 47 permanent inhabitants.<sup id="cite_ref-Miscellany_2020_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Miscellany_2020-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="History of the Pitcairn Islands">History of the Pitcairn Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg/220px-Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg/330px-Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg/440px-Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_Island_In_The_Distance.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>West side of the Pitcairn Islands</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairnlanding.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Pitcairnlanding.jpg/220px-Pitcairnlanding.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Pitcairnlanding.jpg/330px-Pitcairnlanding.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Pitcairnlanding.jpg/440px-Pitcairnlanding.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairnlanding.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pitcairn landing</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Adamstown1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Adamstown1.jpg/220px-Adamstown1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="151" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Adamstown1.jpg/330px-Adamstown1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Adamstown1.jpg/440px-Adamstown1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1431" data-file-height="985" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Adamstown1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Adamstown, the only settlement on the Islands</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Polynesian_settlement_and_extinction">Polynesian settlement and extinction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Polynesian settlement and extinction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The earliest known settlers of the Pitcairn Islands were <a href="/wiki/Polynesians" title="Polynesians">Polynesians</a> who appear to have lived on Pitcairn and Henderson, and on Mangareva Island 540 kilometres (340&#160;mi) to the northwest, for several centuries from at least the 11th century.<sup id="cite_ref-PitcairnFurey_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcairnFurey-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> They traded goods and formed social ties among the three islands despite the long canoe voyages between them, which helped the small populations on each island survive despite their limited resources. Eventually, important natural resources were exhausted, inter-island trade broke down and a period of civil war began on Mangareva, causing the small human populations on Henderson and Pitcairn to be cut off and eventually to become extinct.
</p><p>Although archaeologists believe that Polynesians were living on Pitcairn as late as the 15th century,<sup id="cite_ref-PitcairnFurey_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcairnFurey-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> the islands were uninhabited when they were rediscovered by Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="European_arrival">European arrival</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: European arrival">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Portuguese sailor <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Fernandes_de_Queir%C3%B3s" title="Pedro Fernandes de Queirós">Pedro Fernandes de Queirós</a> came upon Ducie and Henderson Islands while sailing for the Spanish Crown, arriving on 26 January 1606. He named them La Encarnación ("<a href="/wiki/Incarnation_(Christianity)" title="Incarnation (Christianity)">The Incarnation</a>") and San Juan Bautista ("Saint <a href="/wiki/John_the_Baptist" title="John the Baptist">John the Baptist</a>"), respectively. However, some sources express doubt about exactly which of the islands were visited and named by Queirós, suggesting that La Encarnación may actually have been Henderson Island, and San Juan Bautista may have been Pitcairn Island.<sup id="cite_ref-histgovtlaws_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-histgovtlaws-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pitcairn Island was sighted on 3 July 1767 by the crew of the British <a href="/wiki/Sloop-of-war" title="Sloop-of-war">sloop</a> <a href="/wiki/HMS_Swallow_(1745)" title="HMS Swallow (1745)">HMS <i>Swallow</i></a>, commanded by Captain <a href="/wiki/Philip_Carteret" title="Philip Carteret">Philip Carteret</a>. The island was named after midshipman <a href="/wiki/Robert_Pitcairn_(Royal_Navy_officer)" title="Robert Pitcairn (Royal Navy officer)">Robert Pitcairn</a>, a 15-year-old crew member who was the first to sight the island. Robert Pitcairn was a son of British Marine Major <a href="/wiki/John_Pitcairn" title="John Pitcairn">John Pitcairn</a>, who later was killed at the 1775 <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Bunker_Hill" title="Battle of Bunker Hill">Battle of Bunker Hill</a> in the <a href="/wiki/American_Revolution" title="American Revolution">American War of Independence</a>.
</p><p>Carteret, who sailed without the newly invented <a href="/wiki/Marine_chronometer" title="Marine chronometer">marine chronometer</a>, charted the island at <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;params=25_02_S_133_21_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">25°02′S</span> <span class="longitude">133°21′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">25.033°S 133.350°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-25.033; -133.350</span></span></span></a></span>, and although the <a href="/wiki/Latitude" title="Latitude">latitude</a> was reasonably accurate, his recorded longitude was incorrect by about 3°, putting his coordinates 330&#160;km (210&#160;mi) to the west of the actual island. This made Pitcairn difficult to find, as highlighted by the failure of captain <a href="/wiki/James_Cook" title="James Cook">James Cook</a> to locate the island in July 1773.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="European_settlement">European settlement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: European settlement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg/220px-Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="160" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg/330px-Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg/440px-Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="931" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mutiny_HMS_Bounty.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The mutineers turning Bligh and some of the officers and crew adrift from <a href="/wiki/HMS_Bounty" title="HMS Bounty">HMS&#160;<i>Bounty</i></a> on 29 April 1789.</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/HMS_Bounty" title="HMS Bounty">HMS Bounty</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mutiny_on_the_Bounty" title="Mutiny on the Bounty">Mutiny on the Bounty</a></div>
<p>In 1790, nine of the mutineers from the <i>Bounty</i>, along with the native Tahitian men and women who were with them (six men, 11 women, and a baby girl), settled on Pitcairn Island and set fire to the <i>Bounty</i>. The inhabitants of the island were well aware of the <i>Bounty</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em;">&#39;</span>s location, which is still visible underwater in <a href="/wiki/Bounty_Bay" title="Bounty Bay">Bounty Bay</a>, but the wreckage gained significant attention in 1957 when documented by <i><a href="/wiki/National_Geographic_Society" title="National Geographic Society">National Geographic</a></i> explorer <a href="/wiki/Luis_Marden" title="Luis Marden">Luis Marden</a>. Although the settlers survived by farming and fishing, the initial period of settlement was marked by serious tensions among them. <a href="/wiki/Alcoholism" title="Alcoholism">Alcoholism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Murder" title="Murder">murder</a>, disease and other ills took the lives of most mutineers and Tahitian men. <a href="/wiki/John_Adams_(mutineer)" title="John Adams (mutineer)">John Adams</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ned_Young" class="mw-redirect" title="Ned Young">Ned Young</a> turned to the <a href="/wiki/Religious_text" title="Religious text">scriptures</a>, using the ship's <a href="/wiki/Bounty_Bible" title="Bounty Bible">Bible</a> as their guide for a new and peaceful society. Young eventually died of an <a href="/wiki/Asthma" title="Asthma">asthmatic</a> infection.
</p><p>Ducie Island was rediscovered in 1791 by Royal Navy captain Edwards aboard <a href="/wiki/HMS_Pandora_(1779)" title="HMS Pandora (1779)">HMS&#160;<i>Pandora</i></a>, while searching for the <i>Bounty</i> mutineers. He named it after <a href="/wiki/Francis_Reynolds-Moreton,_3rd_Baron_Ducie" title="Francis Reynolds-Moreton, 3rd Baron Ducie">Francis Reynolds-Moreton, 3rd Baron Ducie</a>, also a captain in the Royal Navy.
</p><p>The Pitcairn islanders reported it was not until 27 December 1795 that the first ship since the <i>Bounty</i> was seen from the island, but it did not approach the land and they could not make out the nationality. A second ship appeared in 1801, but made no attempt to communicate with them. A third came sufficiently near to see their house, but did not try to send a boat on shore. Finally, the American sealing ship <i>Topaz</i>, under <a href="/wiki/Mayhew_Folger" title="Mayhew Folger">Mayhew Folger</a>, became the first to visit the island, when the crew spent ten hours on Pitcairn in February 1808.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Whalers subsequently became regular visitors to the island. The last recorded whaler to visit was the <i>James Arnold</i> in 1888.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island,_South_Seas,_1814,_J._Shillibeer.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island%2C_South_Seas%2C_1814%2C_J._Shillibeer.jpg/220px-A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island%2C_South_Seas%2C_1814%2C_J._Shillibeer.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island%2C_South_Seas%2C_1814%2C_J._Shillibeer.jpg/330px-A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island%2C_South_Seas%2C_1814%2C_J._Shillibeer.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island%2C_South_Seas%2C_1814%2C_J._Shillibeer.jpg/440px-A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island%2C_South_Seas%2C_1814%2C_J._Shillibeer.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1400" data-file-height="790" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:A_view_of_Pitcairn%27s_Island,_South_Seas,_1814,_J._Shillibeer.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>View of Pitcairn's Island, South Seas, 1814, J. Shillibeer</div></div></div>
<p>A report of Folger's discovery was forwarded to the <a href="/wiki/British_Admiralty" class="mw-redirect" title="British Admiralty">Admiralty</a>, mentioning the mutineers and giving a more precise location of the island: <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;params=25_02_S_130_00_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">25°02′S</span> <span class="longitude">130°00′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">25.033°S 130.000°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-25.033; -130.000</span></span></span></a></span>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> However, this was not known to Sir <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Staines" title="Thomas Staines">Thomas Staines</a>, who commanded a Royal Navy <a href="/wiki/Flotilla" title="Flotilla">flotilla</a> of two ships, HMS <i>Briton</i> and HMS <i>Tagus</i>, which found the island at <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;params=25_04_S_130_25_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">25°04′S</span> <span class="longitude">130°25′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">25.067°S 130.417°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-25.067; -130.417</span></span></span></a></span> (by meridian observation) on 17 September 1814. Staines sent a party ashore and wrote a detailed report for the Admiralty.<sup id="cite_ref-hist_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hist-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> By that time, only one mutineer, John Adams, remained alive. He was granted amnesty for his part in the mutiny.<sup id="cite_ref-hist_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hist-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Henderson Island was rediscovered on 17 January 1819 by British Captain James Henderson of the British <a href="/wiki/East_India_Company" title="East India Company">East India Company</a> ship <i>Hercules</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Captain Henry King, sailing on <i>Elizabeth</i>, landed on 2 March to find the king's <a href="/wiki/Military_colours,_standards_and_guidons" title="Military colours, standards and guidons">colours</a> already flying. His crew scratched the name of their ship into a tree. Oeno Island was discovered on 26 January 1824 by American captain George Worth aboard the whaler <a href="/wiki/Oeno_(ship)" title="Oeno (ship)"><i>Oeno</i></a>.
</p><p>In 1832, having tried and failed to petition the British government and the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a>, <a href="/wiki/Joshua_Hill_(Pitcairn_Island_leader)" title="Joshua Hill (Pitcairn Island leader)">Joshua Hill</a>, an American adventurer, arrived. He reported that by March 1833, he had founded a Temperance Society to combat drunkenness, a "<a href="/wiki/Maundy_Thursday" title="Maundy Thursday">Maundy Thursday Society</a>", a monthly prayer meeting, a juvenile society, a Peace Society and a school.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="British_colony">British colony</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: British colony">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/British_Western_Pacific_Territories" title="British Western Pacific Territories">British Western Pacific Territories</a></div>
<p>Traditionally, Pitcairn Islanders consider that their islands officially became a British colony on 30 November 1838, at the same time becoming <a href="/wiki/Timeline_of_women%27s_suffrage" title="Timeline of women&#39;s suffrage">one of the first territories to extend voting rights to women</a>. By the mid-1850s, the Pitcairn community was outgrowing the island; its leaders appealed to the British government for assistance, and were offered <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island" title="Norfolk Island">Norfolk Island</a>. On 3 May 1856, the entire population of 193 people set sail for Norfolk on board the <i>Morayshire</i>, arriving on 8 June after a difficult five-week trip. However, just 18 months later, 17 of the Pitcairn Islanders returned to their home island, and another 27 followed five years later.<sup id="cite_ref-hist_17-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hist-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/HMS_Thetis_(1871)" title="HMS Thetis (1871)">HMS&#160;<i>Thetis</i></a> visited Pitcairn Island on 18 April 1881 and "found the people very happy and contented, and in perfect health". At that time the population was 96, an increase of six since the visit of <a href="/wiki/Algernon_Frederick_Rous_de_Horsey" class="mw-redirect" title="Algernon Frederick Rous de Horsey">Admiral de Horsey</a> in September 1878. Stores had recently been delivered from friends in England, including two whale-boats and <a href="/wiki/Portland_cement" title="Portland cement">Portland cement</a>, which was used to make the reservoir watertight. HMS <i>Thetis</i> gave the islanders 200&#160;lb (91&#160;kg) of biscuits, 100&#160;lb (45&#160;kg) of candles, and 100&#160;lb of soap and clothing to the value of £31, donated by the <a href="/wiki/Ship%27s_company" title="Ship&#39;s company">ship's company</a>. An American trading ship called <i>Venus</i> had recently bestowed a supply of <a href="/wiki/Cotton" title="Cotton">cotton seed</a>, to provide the islanders with a crop for future trade.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_Islanders,_1916.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Pitcairn_Islanders%2C_1916.jpg/220px-Pitcairn_Islanders%2C_1916.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="137" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Pitcairn_Islanders%2C_1916.jpg/330px-Pitcairn_Islanders%2C_1916.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Pitcairn_Islanders%2C_1916.jpg/440px-Pitcairn_Islanders%2C_1916.jpg 2x" data-file-width="713" data-file-height="443" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_Islanders,_1916.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pitcairn islanders, 1916</div></div></div>
<p>In 1886, the <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventist</a> layman <a href="/wiki/John_Tay" title="John Tay">John Tay</a> visited Pitcairn and persuaded most of the islanders to accept his faith. He returned in 1890 on the missionary schooner <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_(schooner)" title="Pitcairn (schooner)"><i>Pitcairn</i></a> with an ordained minister to perform <a href="/wiki/Baptism" title="Baptism">baptisms</a>. Since then, the majority of Pitcairn Islanders have been Adventists.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The islands of Henderson, Oeno and Ducie were annexed by Britain in 1902: Henderson on 1 July, Oeno on 10 July, and Ducie on 19 December.<sup id="cite_ref-histgovtlaws_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-histgovtlaws-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> In 1938, the three islands, along with Pitcairn, were incorporated into a single administrative unit called the "Pitcairn Group of Islands". The population peaked at 233 in 1937.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> It has since decreased owing to emigration, primarily to Australia and New Zealand.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sexual_abuse_in_modern_times">Sexual abuse in modern times</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Sexual abuse in modern times">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Three cases of imprisonment for sex with underage girls were reported in the 1950s.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1999, Gail Cox, a police officer from <a href="/wiki/Kent" title="Kent">Kent</a>, <a href="/wiki/UK" class="mw-redirect" title="UK">UK</a>, served on a temporary assignment on Pitcairn, and began uncovering allegations of sexual abuse. When a 15-year-old girl decided to press <a href="/wiki/Rape" title="Rape">rape</a> charges in 1999, criminal proceedings (code-named "Operation Unique") were set in motion. The charges include 21 counts of <a href="/wiki/Rape" title="Rape">rape</a>, 41 of <a href="/wiki/Indecent_assault" title="Indecent assault">indecent assault</a>, and two of <a href="/wiki/Indecency_with_Children_Act_1960" title="Indecency with Children Act 1960">gross indecency</a> with a child under 14. Over the following two years, police officers in <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, New Zealand and the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a> interviewed every woman who had lived on Pitcairn in the past 20 years, as well as all of the accused men. The file was held by Pitcairn's first Public Prosecutor Simon Moore, an Auckland Crown Solicitor appointed to the position by the British government for the purposes of the investigation.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australian</a> <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventist</a> pastor Neville Tosen, who spent two years on Pitcairn around the turn of the millennium, said that on his arrival, he had been taken aback by the conduct of the children, but he had not immediately realised what was happening. "I noticed worrying signs such as inexplicable mood swings," he said. "It took me three months to realise they were being abused." Tosen tried to bring the matter before the Island Council (the legislative body which doubles as the island's court), but was rebuffed. One councillor told him, "Look, the <a href="/wiki/Age_of_consent" title="Age of consent">age of consent</a> has always been 12 and it doesn't hurt them."<sup id="cite_ref-Paradise_under_a_cloud_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Paradise_under_a_cloud-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A study of island records confirmed anecdotal evidence that most girls bore their first child between the ages of 12 and 15. "I think the girls were conditioned to accept that it was a man's world and once they turned 12, they were eligible," Tosen said. Mothers and grandmothers were resigned to the situation, telling him that their own childhood experience had been the same; they regarded it as just a part of life on Pitcairn. One grandmother wondered what all the fuss was about. Tosen was convinced, however, that the early sexual experience was very damaging to the girls, outright stating, "They can't settle or form solid relationships. They did suffer, no doubt about it."<sup id="cite_ref-Paradise_under_a_cloud_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Paradise_under_a_cloud-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Sexual_assault_trials_of_2004">Sexual assault trials of 2004</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Sexual assault trials of 2004">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_sexual_assault_trial_of_2004" class="mw-redirect" title="Pitcairn sexual assault trial of 2004">Pitcairn sexual assault trial of 2004</a></div>
<p>In 2004, charges were laid against seven men living on Pitcairn and six living abroad. This accounted for nearly a third of the male population, and half of the island's adult males. After extensive trials, most of the men were convicted, some with multiple counts of sexual encounters with children.<sup id="cite_ref-Tweedie_2004_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tweedie_2004-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> On 25 October 2004, six men were convicted, including <a href="/wiki/Steve_Christian" title="Steve Christian">Steve Christian</a>, the island's mayor at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-Fickling_2004_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fickling_2004-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> In 2004, the islanders had about 20 firearms among them, which they surrendered ahead of the <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_sexual_assault_trial_of_2004" class="mw-redirect" title="Pitcairn sexual assault trial of 2004">sexual assault trials</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> After the six men lost their final appeal, the British government set up a prison on the island at Bob's Valley.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The men began serving their sentences in late 2006. By 2010, all had served their sentences or been granted home detention status.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2016, <a href="/wiki/Mike_Warren_(mayor)" title="Mike Warren (mayor)">Mike Warren</a>, Pitcairn mayor from 2008 to 2013, was convicted and sentenced to 20 months imprisonment for possession of child pornography.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Geography of the Pitcairn Islands">Geography of the Pitcairn Islands</a></div>
<p>The Pitcairn Islands form the southeasternmost extension of the geological <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelago</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Tuamotus" title="Tuamotus">Tuamotus</a> of <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a>, and consist of four islands: <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Island" title="Pitcairn Island">Pitcairn Island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oeno_Island" title="Oeno Island">Oeno Island</a> (atoll with five islets, one of which is Sandy Island), <a href="/wiki/Henderson_Island_(Pitcairn_Islands)" title="Henderson Island (Pitcairn Islands)">Henderson Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ducie_Island" title="Ducie Island">Ducie Island</a> (atoll with four islets).
</p><p>The Pitcairn Islands were formed by a centre of upwelling <a href="/wiki/Magma" title="Magma">magma</a> called the <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_hotspot" title="Pitcairn hotspot">Pitcairn hotspot</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Island" title="Pitcairn Island">Pitcairn Island</a> is a volcanic remnant primarily formed of <a href="/wiki/Tuff" title="Tuff">tuff</a>, where the north side of the cone has been eroded.<sup id="cite_ref-PitcairnFurey_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcairnFurey-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Pitcairn is the only permanently inhabited island. Adamstown, the main settlement on the island, lies within the volcanic basin.<sup id="cite_ref-PitcairnFurey_9-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcairnFurey-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Pitcairn is accessible only by boat through Bounty Bay, due to the island's steep cliffs.<sup id="cite_ref-PitcairnFurey_9-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcairnFurey-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Henderson Island, covering about 86% of the territory's total land area and supporting a rich variety of animals in its nearly inaccessible interior, is also capable of supporting a small human population despite its scarce fresh water, but access is difficult, owing to its outer shores being steep limestone cliffs covered by sharp coral. In 1988, this island was designated as a UNESCO World Heritage site.<sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The other islands are at a distance of more than 100&#160;km (62&#160;mi) and are not habitable.
</p><p>Pitcairn Island has no permanent water source; however, the island has three seasonal semi-permanent springs.<sup id="cite_ref-PitcairnFurey_9-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PitcairnFurey-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg/220px-Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="209" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg/330px-Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg/440px-Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="972" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Geodesy_Collection_Pitcairn_Island.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Geodesy" title="Geodesy">Geodesy</a> operations on the Pitcairn Islands</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Climate of the Pitcairn Islands">Climate of the Pitcairn Islands</a></div>
<p>Pitcairn is located just south of the Tropic of Capricorn and experiences year-round warm weather.
</p>
