</div><p><br />
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>About nine plant species are thought to <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">occur only</a> on Pitcairn. These include tapau, formerly an important timber resource, and the <a href="/wiki/Angiopteris" title="Angiopteris">giant nehe fern</a>. Some, such as red berry (<i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Coprosma_rapensis&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Coprosma rapensis (page does not exist)">Coprosma rapensis</a></i> var. <i>Benefica</i>), are perilously close to extinction.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The plant species <i><a href="/wiki/Glochidion_pitcairnense" title="Glochidion pitcairnense">Glochidion pitcairnense</a></i> is endemic to Pitcairn and Henderson Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Pitcairn is part of the <a href="/wiki/Tuamotu_tropical_moist_forests" title="Tuamotu tropical moist forests">Tuamotu tropical moist forests</a> terrestrial ecoregion.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="List of birds of the Pitcairn Islands">List of birds of the Pitcairn Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Pitcairn" title="List of mammals of Pitcairn">List of mammals of Pitcairn</a></div>
<p>Between 1937 and 1951, <a href="/wiki/Irving_Johnson" title="Irving Johnson">Irving Johnson</a>, skipper of the 29-metre (96&#160;ft) brigantine <i>Yankee Five</i>, introduced five <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_tortoise" title="Galápagos tortoise">Galápagos giant tortoises</a> to Pitcairn. Turpen, also known as <a href="/wiki/Mr_Turpen" title="Mr Turpen">Mr Turpen</a>, or Mr. T, is the sole survivor. Turpen usually lives at Tedside by Western Harbour. A protection order makes it an offence should anyone kill, injure, capture, maim, or cause harm or distress to the tortoise.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The birds of Pitcairn fall into several groups. These include seabirds, wading birds and a small number of resident land-bird species. Of 20 breeding species, Henderson Island has 16, including the unique flightless <a href="/wiki/Henderson_crake" title="Henderson crake">Henderson crake</a>; Oeno hosts 12; Ducie 13 and Pitcairn six species. Birds breeding on Pitcairn include the <a href="/wiki/Fairy_tern" title="Fairy tern">fairy tern</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_noddy" title="Brown noddy">common noddy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Red-tailed_tropicbird" title="Red-tailed tropicbird">red-tailed tropicbird</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_reed_warbler" title="Pitcairn reed warbler">Pitcairn reed warbler</a>, known by Pitcairners as a "sparrow", is endemic to Pitcairn Island; formerly common, it was added to the <a href="/wiki/Endangered_species" title="Endangered species">endangered species</a> list in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A small population of <a href="/wiki/Humpback_whale" title="Humpback whale">humpback whales</a> (which has been poorly studied by scientists) migrate to the islands annually, to <a href="/wiki/Overwintering" title="Overwintering">over-winter</a> and breed.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Important_bird_areas">Important bird areas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Important bird areas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The four islands in the Pitcairn group have been identified by <a href="/wiki/BirdLife_International" title="BirdLife International">BirdLife International</a> as separate <a href="/wiki/Important_Bird_Area" title="Important Bird Area">Important Bird Areas</a> (IBAs). Pitcairn Island is recognised because it is the only nesting site of the <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_reed_warbler" title="Pitcairn reed warbler">Pitcairn reed warbler</a>. Henderson Island is important for its endemic land-birds as well as its breeding <a href="/wiki/Seabird" title="Seabird">seabirds</a>. Oeno's ornithological significance derives principally from its <a href="/wiki/Murphy%27s_petrel" title="Murphy&#39;s petrel">Murphy's petrel</a> colony. Ducie is important for its colonies of <a href="/wiki/Murphy%27s_petrel" title="Murphy&#39;s petrel">Murphy's</a>, <a href="/wiki/Herald_petrel" title="Herald petrel">herald</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kermadec_petrel" title="Kermadec petrel">Kermadec petrels</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Christmas_shearwater" title="Christmas shearwater">Christmas shearwaters</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pitcairn_Islands_Marine_Reserve">Pitcairn Islands Marine Reserve</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Pitcairn Islands Marine Reserve">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In March 2015 the British government established <a href="/wiki/List_of_largest_protected_areas" title="List of largest protected areas">one of the largest</a> <a href="/wiki/Marine_protected_area" title="Marine protected area">marine protected areas</a> in the world around the Pitcairn Islands. The reserve covers the islands' entire <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a>—834,334 square kilometres (322,138&#160;sq&#160;mi). The intention is to protect some of the world's most pristine ocean habitat from <a href="/wiki/Illegal_fishing" class="mw-redirect" title="Illegal fishing">illegal fishing</a> activities. A satellite "watchroom" dubbed Project Eyes on the Seas has been established by the <a href="/wiki/UK_Space_Agency" title="UK Space Agency">Satellite Applications Catapult</a> and <a href="/wiki/The_Pew_Charitable_Trusts" title="The Pew Charitable Trusts">the Pew Charitable Trusts</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Harwell_Science_and_Innovation_Campus" title="Harwell Science and Innovation Campus">Harwell Science and Innovation Campus</a> in <a href="/wiki/Harwell,_Oxfordshire" title="Harwell, Oxfordshire">Harwell, Oxfordshire</a> to monitor vessel activity and to gather the information needed to prosecute unauthorised trawling.<sup id="cite_ref-Gauke_2015_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gauke_2015-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pitcairn_Islands_Dark_Sky_Sanctuary">Pitcairn Islands Dark Sky Sanctuary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Pitcairn Islands Dark Sky Sanctuary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In March 2019 the <a href="/wiki/International_Dark-Sky_Association" title="International Dark-Sky Association">International Dark-Sky Association</a> approved the Pitcairn Islands as a Dark Sky Sanctuary. The sanctuary encompasses all 4 islands in the Pitcairn Islands Group for a total land area of 43.25&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (16<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1020198016">.mw-parser-output .frac{white-space:nowrap}.mw-parser-output .frac .num,.mw-parser-output .frac .den{font-size:80%;line-height:0;vertical-align:super}.mw-parser-output .frac .den{vertical-align:sub}.mw-parser-output .sr-only{border:0;clip:rect(0,0,0,0);height:1px;margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;padding:0;position:absolute;width:1px}</style><span class="frac" role="math"><span class="num">3</span>&#8260;<span class="den">4</span></span> sq. mi.).<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Politics of the Pitcairn Islands">Politics of the Pitcairn Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="302" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1073" data-file-height="1471" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2019_Reuni%C3%A3o_Bilateral_com_o_Pr%C3%ADncipe_Charles_-_48948389972_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Charles III is the head of state of the Pitcairn Islands.</div></div></div>
<p>The Pitcairn Islands are a <a href="/wiki/British_overseas_territory" class="mw-redirect" title="British overseas territory">British overseas territory</a> with a degree of local government. The <a href="/wiki/King_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="King of the United Kingdom">King of the United Kingdom</a> is represented by a Governor, who also holds office as <a href="/wiki/List_of_High_Commissioners_of_the_United_Kingdom_to_New_Zealand" title="List of High Commissioners of the United Kingdom to New Zealand">British High Commissioner</a> to <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> and is based in <a href="/wiki/Wellington" title="Wellington">Wellington</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2010 constitution gives authority for the islands to operate as a <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democracy</a>, with the United Kingdom retaining responsibility for matters such as defence and foreign affairs. The Governor and the Island Council may enact laws for the "<a href="/wiki/Peace,_order_and_good_government" class="mw-redirect" title="Peace, order and good government">peace, order and good government</a>" of Pitcairn. The <a href="/wiki/Island_Council_(Pitcairn)" title="Island Council (Pitcairn)">Island Council</a> customarily appoints a <a href="/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="List of rulers of the Pitcairn Islands">Mayor of Pitcairn</a> as a day-to-day head of the local administration. There is a Commissioner, appointed by the Governor, who liaises between the Council and the Governor's office.
</p><p>Since 2015, same-sex marriage has been legal on Pitcairn Island, although there are no people on the island known to be in such a relationship.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Pitcairn Islands have the smallest population of any democracy in the world.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Committee_on_Decolonization" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Committee on Decolonization">United Nations Committee on Decolonization</a> includes the Pitcairn Islands on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">United Nations list of non-self-governing territories</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The Pitcairn Islands are an <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territories" title="British Overseas Territories">overseas territory of the United Kingdom</a>; defence is the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/UK_Ministry_of_Defence" class="mw-redirect" title="UK Ministry of Defence">Ministry of Defence</a> and <a href="/wiki/His_Majesty%27s_Armed_Forces" class="mw-redirect" title="His Majesty&#39;s Armed Forces">His Majesty's Armed Forces</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> maintains two <a href="/wiki/Offshore_patrol_vessel" class="mw-redirect" title="Offshore patrol vessel">offshore patrol vessels</a> in the Indo-Pacific region, <a href="/wiki/HMS_Tamar_(P233)" title="HMS Tamar (P233)">HMS&#160;<i>Tamar</i></a> and <a href="/wiki/HMS_Spey_(P234)" title="HMS Spey (P234)">HMS&#160;<i>Spey</i></a>. Either may be periodically employed for sovereignty protection and other duties around Pitcairn and her associated islands.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Mandatory_work">Mandatory work</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Mandatory work">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Due to there being no income or sales tax, Pitcairn has established a system of <a href="/wiki/Civil_conscription" title="Civil conscription">civil conscription</a> whereby all able-bodied people are required to perform, when called upon, jobs such as road maintenance and repairs to public buildings.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_Islands_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Pitcairn Islands exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Agriculture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The fertile soil of the Pitcairn valleys, such as Isaac's Valley on the gentle slopes southeast of <a href="/wiki/Adamstown,_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Adamstown, Pitcairn Islands">Adamstown</a>, produces a wide variety of fruits, including bananas (Pitkern: <i>plun</i>), <a href="/wiki/Papaya" title="Papaya">papaya</a> (<i>paw paws</i>), <a href="/wiki/Pineapple" title="Pineapple">pineapples</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mango" title="Mango">mangoes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Watermelon" title="Watermelon">watermelons</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cantaloupe" title="Cantaloupe">cantaloupes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Passionfruit" class="mw-redirect" title="Passionfruit">passionfruit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Breadfruit" title="Breadfruit">breadfruit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconuts</a>, <a href="/wiki/Avocado" title="Avocado">avocadoes</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Citrus" title="Citrus">citrus</a> (including <a href="/wiki/Mandarin_orange" title="Mandarin orange">mandarin oranges</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grapefruit" title="Grapefruit">grapefruit</a>, lemons and <a href="/wiki/Lime_(fruit)" title="Lime (fruit)">limes</a>). Vegetables include <a href="/wiki/Sweet_potato" title="Sweet potato">sweet potatoes</a> (kumura), carrots, <a href="/wiki/Sweet_corn" title="Sweet corn">sweet corn</a>, tomatoes, <a href="/wiki/Taro" title="Taro">taro</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yam_(vegetable)" title="Yam (vegetable)">yams</a>, <a href="/wiki/Peas" class="mw-redirect" title="Peas">peas</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bean" title="Bean">beans</a>. <a href="/wiki/Arrowroot" title="Arrowroot">Arrowroot</a> (<i>Maranta arundinacea</i>) and <a href="/wiki/Sugarcane" title="Sugarcane">sugarcane</a> are grown and harvested to produce arrowroot flour and <a href="/wiki/Molasses" title="Molasses">molasses</a>, respectively. Pitcairn Island is remarkably productive and its benign climate supports a wide range of tropical and temperate crops.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> All land allocation for any use including agriculture is under the discretion of the government. If the government deems agricultural production excessive then it may tax the land. If the agricultural land has been deemed not up to the standards of the government it may confiscate and transfer the land without compensation.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Fish are plentiful in the seas around Pitcairn. <a href="/wiki/Spiny_lobster" title="Spiny lobster">Spiny lobster</a> and a large variety of fish are caught for meals and for trading aboard passing ships. Almost every day someone will go fishing, whether it is from the rocks, from a longboat, or diving with a spear gun. There are numerous types of fish around the island. Fish such as nanwee, white fish, moi, and opapa are caught in shallow water, while snapper, big eye, and cod are caught in deep water, and yellow tail and wahoo are caught by trawling.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Minerals">Minerals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Minerals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Manganese, iron, copper, gold, silver and zinc have been discovered within the <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a>, which extends 370&#160;km (230&#160;mi) offshore and comprises 880,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (340,000&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Honey_production">Honey production</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Honey production">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1998 the UK's overseas aid agency, the <a href="/wiki/Department_for_International_Development" title="Department for International Development">Department for International Development</a>, funded an <a href="/wiki/Apiculture" class="mw-redirect" title="Apiculture">apiculture</a> programme for Pitcairn which included training for Pitcairn's <a href="/wiki/Beekeeper" title="Beekeeper">beekeepers</a> and a detailed analysis of Pitcairn's bees and honey with particular regard to the presence or absence of disease. Pitcairn has one of the best examples of disease-free bee populations anywhere in the world and the honey produced was and remains exceptionally high in quality. Pitcairn bees are also a placid variety and, within a short time, beekeepers are able to work with them wearing minimal protection.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> As a result, Pitcairn exports honey to New Zealand and to the United Kingdom. In London, <a href="/wiki/Fortnum_%26_Mason" title="Fortnum &amp; Mason">Fortnum &amp; Mason</a> sells it and it is reportedly a favourite of <a href="/wiki/Charles_III" title="Charles III">King Charles</a> and formerly <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_II" title="Elizabeth II">Queen Elizabeth</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The Pitcairn Islanders, under the "Bounty Products" and "Delectable Bounty" brands, also export dried fruit including bananas, papayas, pineapples, and mangoes to New Zealand.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Honey production and all honey-related products are a protected monopoly.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> All funds and management are under the supervision and discretion of the government.<sup id="cite_ref-pitcairn.pn_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pitcairn.pn-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Cuisine is not very developed because of Pitcairn's small population. The most traditional meal is pota, mash from <a href="/wiki/Palm_leaves" class="mw-redirect" title="Palm leaves">palm leaves</a> and <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Domestic <a href="/wiki/Tropical_plants" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical plants">tropical plants</a> are abundantly used. These include <a href="/wiki/Basil" title="Basil">basil</a>, <a href="/wiki/Breadfruit" title="Breadfruit">breadfruit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sugar_cane" class="mw-redirect" title="Sugar cane">sugar cane</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bananas" class="mw-redirect" title="Bananas">bananas</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beans" class="mw-redirect" title="Beans">beans</a>. <a href="/wiki/Meat" title="Meat">Meat</a> courses consist mainly of <a href="/wiki/Fish" title="Fish">fish</a> and <a href="/wiki/Beef" title="Beef">beef</a>. Given that most of the population's ancestry is from the UK, the cuisine is influenced by <a href="/wiki/British_cuisine" title="British cuisine">British cuisine</a>; for example, the <a href="/wiki/Meat_pie" title="Meat pie">meat pie</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The cuisine of <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island" title="Norfolk Island">Norfolk Island</a> is very similar to that of the Pitcairn Islands, as Norfolk Islanders trace their origins to Pitcairn. The local cuisine is a blend of <a href="/wiki/British_cuisine" title="British cuisine">British cuisine</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Tahitian_cuisine&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Tahitian cuisine (page does not exist)">Tahitian cuisine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Recipes from Norfolk Island of Pitcairn origin include mudda (green banana dumplings) and kumara pilhi.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The island's cuisine also includes foods not found on Pitcairn, such as chopped salads and fruit pies.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Tourism plays a major role on Pitcairn. Tourism is the focus for building the economy. It focuses on small groups coming by charter vessel and staying at "home stays". About ten times a year, passengers from expedition-type cruise ships come ashore for a day, weather permitting.<sup id="cite_ref-FCO_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FCO-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> As of 2019, the government has been operating the MV <i>Silver Supporter</i> as the island's only dedicated passenger/cargo vessel, providing adventure tourism holidays to Pitcairn every week. Tourists stay with local families and experience the island's culture while contributing to the local economy. Providing accommodation is a growing source of revenue, and some families have invested in private self-contained units adjacent to their homes for tourists to rent.
</p><p>Entry requirements for short stays, up to 14 days, which do not require a visa, and for longer stays, that do require prior clearance, are explained in official documents.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> All persons under 16 years of age require prior clearance before landing, irrespective of the length of stay.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Crafts_and_external_sales">Crafts and external sales</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Crafts and external sales">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Postage_stamps_and_postal_history_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Postage stamps and postal history of the Pitcairn Islands">Postage stamps and postal history of the Pitcairn Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg/220px-Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg/330px-Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg/440px-Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg 2x" data-file-width="942" data-file-height="626" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_1940_07.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Postage_stamps_and_postal_history_of_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Postage stamps and postal history of the Pitcairn Islands">Stamp of the Pitcairn Islands</a></div></div></div>
<p>The government holds a monopoly over "any article of whatsoever nature made, manufactured, prepared for sale or produced by any of the inhabitants of Pitcairn Island".<sup id="cite_ref-pitcairn.pn_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pitcairn.pn-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The flow of funds from these revenue sources are from customer to the government to the Pitcairners.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_70-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> The Pitcairners are involved in creating crafts and curios (made out of wood from <a href="/wiki/Henderson_Island_(Pitcairn_Islands)" title="Henderson Island (Pitcairn Islands)">Henderson</a>). Typical woodcarvings include sharks, fish, whales, dolphins, turtles, vases, birds, walking sticks, book boxes, and models of the <i>Bounty</i>. Miro (<i><a href="/wiki/Thespesia_populnea" title="Thespesia populnea">Thespesia populnea</a></i>), a dark and durable wood, is preferred for carving. Islanders also produce <a href="/wiki/Tapa_cloth" title="Tapa cloth">tapa cloth</a> and painted Hattie leaves.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The major sources of revenue have been the sale of coins and postage stamps to collectors, <a href="/wiki/.pn" title=".pn">.pn</a> domain names, and the sale of handicrafts to passing ships, most of which are on the United Kingdom to New Zealand route via the <a href="/wiki/Panama_Canal" title="Panama Canal">Panama Canal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> The Pitcairn Islands issued their first stamp in 1940. These became very popular with <a href="/wiki/Stamp_collecting" title="Stamp collecting">stamp collectors</a>, and their sale became the dominant source of revenue for the community. Profits went into a general fund which enabled the island to be mostly self-sufficient. This fund was used to meet the regular needs of the community, and pay wages. Funds in excess of regular expenses were used to build a school and hire a teacher from New Zealand, the first professional teacher hired on the island. The fund was also used to subsidise imports and travel to New Zealand. At later points, the sale of coins and .pn domain names also contributed to the fund. Towards the end of the 20th century, as writing letters became less common and stamp collecting became less popular, revenue for the fund declined.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> In 2004, the island went bankrupt, with the British government subsequently providing 90% of its annual budget.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Electricity">Electricity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Electricity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Diesel generators provide the island with electricity from 7 am to 10 pm. A wind power plant was planned to be installed to help reduce the high cost of power generation associated with the import of diesel, but was cancelled in 2013 after a project overrun of three years and a cost of £250,000.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The only qualified high-voltage electrician on Pitcairn, who manages the electricity grid, reached the age of 67 in 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-government.pn_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.pn-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Islanders" title="Pitcairn Islanders">Pitcairn Islanders</a></div>
<p>The islands have suffered a substantial population decline since 1940, and the viability of the island's community is in doubt (see <a href="#Potential_extinction">§ Potential extinction</a>, below). The government has tried to attract migrants. However, these initiatives have not been effective.<sup id="cite_ref-star_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-star-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Only two children were born on Pitcairn in the 21 years prior to 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> In 2005, Shirley and Simon Young became the first married outsider couple in history to obtain citizenship on Pitcairn.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Language">Language</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Language">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Most resident Pitcairn Islanders are <a href="/wiki/Descendants_of_the_Bounty_mutineers" title="Descendants of the Bounty mutineers">descendants of the <i>Bounty</i> mutineers</a> and Tahitians (or other Polynesians). <a href="/wiki/Pitkern" class="mw-redirect" title="Pitkern">Pitkern</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Creole_language" title="Creole language">creole language</a> derived from 18th-century English, with elements of the <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian language</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Britannica_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> It is spoken as a first language by the population and is taught alongside English at the island's only school. It is closely related to the creole language <a href="/wiki/Norfuk_language" title="Norfuk language">Norfuk</a>, spoken on <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island" title="Norfolk Island">Norfolk Island</a>, because Norfolk was repopulated in the mid-19th century by Pitcairners.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The only church building on the island is <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventist</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_26-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The Seventh-day Adventist Church is not a <a href="/wiki/State_religion" title="State religion">state religion</a>, as no laws concerning its establishment were passed by the <a href="/wiki/Local_government" title="Local government">local government</a>. A successful Seventh-day Adventist mission in the 1890s was important in shaping Pitcairn society. In recent years, the church population has declined, and as of 2000<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, eight of the then forty islanders attended services regularly,<sup id="cite_ref-ANN_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ANN-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> but most attend church on special occasions. From Friday at sunset until Saturday at sunset, Pitcairners observe a day of rest in observance of the <a href="/wiki/Sabbath_and_Seventh-day_Adventism" class="mw-redirect" title="Sabbath and Seventh-day Adventism">Sabbath</a>, or as a mark of respect for observant Adventists.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Adamstown" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg/220px-Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="151" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg/330px-Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg/440px-Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1496" data-file-height="1026" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pitcairn_-_Church_of_Adamstown.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Church of Adamstown</div></div></div>
<p>The church was built in 1954 and is run by the Church board and resident pastor, who usually serves a two-year term. The Sabbath School meets at 10 am on Saturday mornings, and is followed by Divine Service an hour later. On Tuesday evenings, there is another service in the form of a prayer meeting.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Education is free and compulsory between the ages of five and 15.<sup id="cite_ref-PUCEd_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PUCEd-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Children up to the age of 12 are taught at <a href="/wiki/Pulau_School" title="Pulau School">Pulau School</a>, while children of 13 and over attend secondary school in New Zealand, or are educated via correspondence school.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The island's children have produced a book in <a href="/wiki/Pitkern_language" title="Pitkern language">Pitkern</a> and English called <i>Mi Bas Side orn Pitcairn</i> or <i>My Favourite Place on Pitcairn</i>.
</p><p>The school on Pitcairn, Pulau School, provides pre-school and primary education based on the New Zealand syllabus. The teacher is appointed by the governor from suitable qualified applicants who are registered in New Zealand as teachers. The government officially took responsibility for education in 1958; the Seventh-day Adventist Church had done so from the 1890s until 1958. There were ten students in 1999; enrollment was previously 20 in the early 1950s, 28 in 1959, and 36 in 1962. The Pulau School has a residence for teachers built in 2004; there was a previous such facility built in 1950.<sup id="cite_ref-PUCEd_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PUCEd-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Historical_population">Historical population</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Historical population">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pitcairn's population has significantly decreased since its peak of over 200 in the 1930s, to less than fifty permanent residents today (2021).<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Potential_extinction">Potential extinction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Potential extinction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As of April&#160;2021<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the total resident population of the Pitcairn Islands was 47.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> It is rare for all the residents to be on-island at the same time; it is common for several residents to be off-island for varying lengths of time visiting family, for medical reasons, or to attend international conferences. A <a href="/wiki/Diaspora" title="Diaspora">diaspora</a> survey projected that by 2045, if nothing were done, only three people of working age would be left on the island, with the rest being very old. In addition, the survey revealed that residents who had left the island over the past decades showed little interest in coming back. Of the hundreds of emigrants contacted, only 33 were willing to participate in the survey and just three expressed a desire to return.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>As of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the labour force consisted of 31 able-bodied persons: 17 males and 14 females between 18 and 64 years of age. Of the 31, just seven are younger than 40, but 18 are over the age of 50.<sup id="cite_ref-government.pn_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.pn-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Most of the men undertake the more strenuous physical tasks on the island such as crewing the longboats, cargo handling, and the operation and maintenance of physical assets. Longboat crew retirement age is 58. There were then 12 men aged between 18 and 58 residing on Pitcairn. Each <a href="/wiki/Longboat" title="Longboat">longboat</a> requires a minimum crew of three; of the four longboat <a href="/wiki/Coxswain" title="Coxswain">coxswains</a>, two were in their late 50s.<sup id="cite_ref-government.pn_88-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.pn-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Pitcairn government's attempts to attract migrants have been unsuccessful. Since 2013, some 700 make inquiries each year, but so far, not a single formal settlement application has been received.<sup id="cite_ref-government.pn_88-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.pn-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-star_89-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-star-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The migrants are prohibited from taking local jobs or claiming benefits for a certain length of time, even those with children.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> The migrants are expected to have at least <a href="/wiki/NZ$" class="mw-redirect" title="NZ$">NZ$</a>30,000 per person in savings and are expected to build their own house at average cost of NZ$140,000.<sup id="cite_ref-billhaigh_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-billhaigh-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Menzies_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Menzies-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> It is also possible to bring off-island builders at an additional cost of between NZ$23,000 and NZ$28,000.<sup id="cite_ref-Menzies_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Menzies-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The average annual cost of living on the island is <a href="/wiki/NZ$" class="mw-redirect" title="NZ$">NZ$</a>9,464.<sup id="cite_ref-billhaigh_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-billhaigh-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> There is, however, no assurance of the migrant's right to remain on Pitcairn; after their first two years, the council must review and reapprove the migrant's status.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The migrants are also required to take part in the unpaid public work to keep the island in order such as maintaining the island's numerous roads and paths, building roads, navigating the island longboats, and cleaning public toilets.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> There are also restrictions on bringing children under the age of 16 to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Gay_2013_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gay_2013-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-www.gov.uk_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www.gov.uk-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Freight from <a href="/wiki/Tauranga" title="Tauranga">Tauranga</a> to Pitcairn on the <span class="nowrap">MV <i>Claymore II</i></span> (Pitcairn Island's dedicated passenger and cargo ship chartered by the Pitcairn government) is charged at NZ$350/m<sup>3</sup> for Pitcairners and NZ$1,000/m<sup>3</sup> for all other freight.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, Pitcairners are charged NZ$3,000 for a one-way trip; others are charged NZ$5,000.<sup id="cite_ref-government.pn_88-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.pn-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the government's Pitcairn Islands Economic Report stated that "&#91;no one&#93; will migrate to Pitcairn Islands for economic reasons as there are limited government jobs, a lack of private sector employment, as well as considerable competition for the tourism dollar." The Pitcairners take turns to accommodate those few tourists who occasionally visit the island.<sup id="cite_ref-government.pn_88-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-government.pn-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As the island remains a <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territories" title="British Overseas Territories">British Overseas Territory</a>, the British government will at some stage be required to make a decision about the island's future.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The once-strict moral codes, which prohibited dancing, <a href="/wiki/Public_displays_of_affection" class="mw-redirect" title="Public displays of affection">public displays of affection</a>, smoking, and consumption of alcohol, have been relaxed. Islanders and visitors no longer require a six-month licence to purchase, import, and consume alcohol.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> There is now one licensed café and bar on the island, and the government store sells alcohol and cigarettes.
</p><p>Fishing and swimming are two popular recreational activities. A birthday celebration or the arrival of a ship or yacht will involve the entire Pitcairn community in a public dinner in the Square, Adamstown. Tables are covered in a variety of foods, including fish, meat, chicken, pilhi, baked rice, boiled <i>plun</i> (banana), breadfruit, vegetable dishes, an assortment of pies, bread, breadsticks, an array of desserts, pineapple, and watermelon.
</p><p>Paid employees maintain the island's numerous roads and paths. As of 2011<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the island had a <a href="/wiki/Labour_force" class="mw-redirect" title="Labour force">labour force</a> of over 35 men and women.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_26-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Bounty_Day" title="Bounty Day">Bounty Day</a> is an annual <a href="/wiki/Public_holiday" title="Public holiday">public holiday</a> celebrated on Pitcairn on 23 January<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> to commemorate the day in 1790 when the mutineers arrived on the island in <a href="/wiki/HMS_Bounty" title="HMS Bounty">HMS <i>Bounty</i></a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Media_and_communications">Media and communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Media and communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<dl><dt>Post</dt></dl>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Postal_codes_in_the_Pitcairn_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Postal codes in the Pitcairn Islands">UK Postcode</a> for directing mail to Pitcairn Island is PCRN 1ZZ.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<dl><dt>Newspapers</dt></dl>
<p><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.miscellany.pn">The Pitcairn Miscellany</a> is a monthly newspaper available in print and online editions.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dem_Tull" class="mw-redirect" title="Dem Tull">Dem Tull</a> was an online monthly newsletter published between 2007 and 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<dl><dt>Telecommunications</dt></dl>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_the_Pitcairn_Islands" title="Telephone numbers in the Pitcairn Islands">Telephone numbers in the Pitcairn Islands</a></div>
<p>Pitcairn uses New Zealand's <a href="/wiki/List_of_country_calling_codes" title="List of country calling codes">international calling code</a>, <a href="/wiki/%2B64" class="mw-redirect" title="+64">+64</a>. It is still on the <a href="/wiki/Telephone_exchange#Manual_service_exchanges" title="Telephone exchange">manual telephone system</a>.
</p>
<dl><dt>Radio</dt></dl>
<p>There is no broadcast station. Marine band <a href="/wiki/Walkie-talkie" title="Walkie-talkie">walkie-talkie</a> radios are used to maintain contact among people in different areas of the island. Foreign stations can be picked up on <a href="/wiki/Shortwave_radio" title="Shortwave radio">shortwave radio</a>.
</p>
<dl><dt>Amateur radio</dt></dl>
<p>Callsign website <a href="/wiki/QRZ.COM" class="mw-redirect" title="QRZ.COM">QRZ.COM</a> lists six <a href="/wiki/Amateur_radio" title="Amateur radio">amateur radio</a> operators on the island, using the <a href="/wiki/ITU_prefix" title="ITU prefix">ITU prefix</a> (assigned through the UK) of VP6, two of whom have a second VR6 callsign. However, two of these 6 are listed by QRZ.COM as deceased, while others are no longer active. Pitcairn Island has one callsign allocated to its Club Station, VP6PAC.
</p><p>QRZ.COM lists 29 VP6 callsigns being allocated in total, 20 of them to off-islanders. Of these, five were allocated to temporary residents and ten to individuals visiting. The rest were assigned to the <a href="/wiki/DX-pedition" title="DX-pedition">DX-peditions</a> to Pitcairn, one of which took place in 2012<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> In 2008, a major DX-pedition visited <a href="/wiki/Ducie_Island" title="Ducie Island">Ducie Island</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> In 2018, another major DX-pedition visited <a href="/wiki/Ducie_Island" title="Ducie Island">Ducie Island</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<dl><dt>Television</dt></dl>
<p>Pitcairn can receive a number of television channels but only has capacity to broadcast two channels to houses at any one time. The channels are currently switched on a regular basis.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<dl><dt>Internet</dt></dl>
<p>There is one government-sponsored <a href="/wiki/Satellite_Internet_access" title="Satellite Internet access">satellite Internet</a> connection, with networking provided to the inhabitants of the island. Pitcairn's <a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">country code top-level domain</a> is <a href="/wiki/.pn" title=".pn">.pn</a>. Residents pay NZ$50 (about £26) for 25 <a href="/wiki/Gigabyte" title="Gigabyte">GB</a> of data per month.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, a single 1 Mbit/s link installed provided the islanders with an Internet connection, the 1 Mbit/s was shared across all families on the island. By December 2017, the British Government implemented a 4G LTE mobile network in Adamstown with shared speeds of 5 Mbit/s across all islanders.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>All settlers of the Pitcairn Islands arrived by boat or ship. Pitcairn Island <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_without_an_airport" title="List of countries without an airport">does not have an airport</a>, airstrip or seaport; the islanders rely on <a href="/wiki/Longboat" title="Longboat">longboats</a> to ferry people and goods between visiting ships and shore through Bounty Bay.<sup id="cite_ref-FCO_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FCO-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Access to the rest of the shoreline is restricted by jagged rocks. The island has one shallow harbour with a launch ramp accessible only by small longboats.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> In 2014, a medical emergency requiring transport to a hospital in <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a> involved a 335 <a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nautical mile</a> (540&#160;km) trip in an <a href="/wiki/Boat" title="Boat">open boat</a> to the island of <a href="/wiki/Mangareva" title="Mangareva">Mangareva</a>, then an <a href="/wiki/Air_medical_services" title="Air medical services">air ambulance</a> flight 975 <a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nautical miles</a> (1570&#160;km) to <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>. It was organized by medical authorities in the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a> and <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>, and <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">French authorities</a> in <a href="/wiki/Mangareva" title="Mangareva">Mangareva</a> and <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>. The <a href="/wiki/List_of_High_Commissioners_of_the_United_Kingdom_to_New_Zealand" title="List of High Commissioners of the United Kingdom to New Zealand">British High Commissioner to New Zealand</a> said "It can be a hazardous sea voyage from Pitcairn to Mangareva. This is especially so for open long boats. However, I'm pleased to say that all went well and both boats arrived safely in Mangareva mid-morning today, New Zealand time."<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A dedicated passenger and cargo supply ship chartered by the Pitcairn Island government, the <span class="nowrap"><a href="/wiki/MV_Claymore_II" class="mw-redirect" title="MV Claymore II">MV <i>Claymore II</i></a></span>, was until 2018 the principal transport from <a href="/wiki/Mangareva" title="Mangareva">Mangareva</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Gambier_Islands" title="Gambier Islands">Gambier Islands</a> of <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a>. The supply ship was replaced in 2019 by <span class="nowrap"><a href="/w/index.php?title=MV_Silver_Supporter&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="MV Silver Supporter (page does not exist)">MV <i>Silver Supporter</i></a></span>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Totegegie_Airport" title="Totegegie Airport">Totegegie Airport</a> in Mangareva can be reached by air from the French Polynesian capital <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is one 6.4-kilometre (4&#160;mi) paved road leading up from Bounty Bay through Adamstown.
</p><p>The main modes of transport on Pitcairn Islands are by four-wheel drive <a href="/wiki/All-terrain_vehicle" title="All-terrain vehicle">quad bikes</a> and on foot.<sup id="cite_ref-FCO_78-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FCO-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Much of the road and track network and some of the footpaths of Pitcairn Island are viewable on <a href="/wiki/Google" title="Google">Google</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Google_Street_View" title="Google Street View">Street View</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_people">Notable people</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Notable people">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Ned_Young" class="mw-redirect" title="Ned Young">Ned Young</a> (b <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 1762, d 1800 on Pitcairn), mutineer from the famous HMS <i>Bounty</i> incident, and co-founder of the mutineers' Pitcairn Island settlement.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Teraura" title="Teraura">Teraura</a> (b <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 1775, d 1850 on Pitcairn), Tahitian noblewoman and tapa weaver, 'partner' of Ned Young, Matthew Quintal and Thursday October Christian I.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/William_McCoy_(mutineer)" class="mw-redirect" title="William McCoy (mutineer)">William McCoy</a> (b <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 1763, d 1798 on Pitcairn), a Scottish sailor and a mutineer on board HMS <i>Bounty</i>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fletcher_Christian" title="Fletcher Christian">Fletcher Christian</a> (b 1764, d 1793 on Pitcairn), Master's mate on board HMS <i>Bounty</i>, died here at age 28.<sup id="cite_ref-Kirk_2012_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kirk_2012-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Matthew_Quintal" class="mw-redirect" title="Matthew Quintal">Matthew Quintal</a> (b 1766, d 1799 on Pitcairn), a Cornish able seaman and mutineer aboard HMS <i>Bounty</i></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Adams_(mutineer)" title="John Adams (mutineer)">John Adams</a> (b 1767, d 1829 on Pitcairn), the last survivor of the HMS <i>Bounty</i> mutineers who settled on Pitcairn Island in January 1790, the year after the mutiny</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Thursday_October_Christian_I" title="Thursday October Christian I">Thursday October Christian I</a> (1790–1831), the first son of <a href="/wiki/Fletcher_Christian" title="Fletcher Christian">Fletcher Christian</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/George_Adams_(magistrate)" title="George Adams (magistrate)">George Adams</a> (1804–1873), served as Chief Magistrate on Pitcairn in 1848</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Thursday_October_Christian_II" title="Thursday October Christian II">Thursday October Christian II</a> (1820–1911), a Pitcairn Islands political leader. Grandson of <a href="/wiki/Fletcher_Christian" title="Fletcher Christian">Fletcher Christian</a> and son of <a href="/wiki/Thursday_October_Christian_I" title="Thursday October Christian I">Thursday October Christian I</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Simon_Young_(magistrate)" title="Simon Young (magistrate)">Simon Young</a> (1823–1893), served as Magistrate of the Pitcairn Islands in 1849</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Moses_Young" title="Moses Young">Moses Young</a> (1829–1909), served as magistrate of Pitcairn Island four times, between 1865 and 1881</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/James_Russell_McCoy" title="James Russell McCoy">James Russell McCoy</a> (1845–1924), served as Magistrate of Pitcairn Island 7 times, between 1870 and 1904</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Stanley_Young" title="Benjamin Stanley Young">Benjamin Stanley Young</a> (1851–1934), served as Magistrate of the Pitcairn Islands twice, from 1884 to 1885, and in 1892</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Rosalind_Amelia_Young" title="Rosalind Amelia Young">Rosalind Amelia Young</a> (1853–1924), a historian from Pitcairn Islands</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/William_Alfred_Young" title="William Alfred Young">William Alfred Young</a> (1863–1911), served as President of the council, and Magistrate of Pitcairn Island three times, between 1897 and 1908</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Matthew_Edmond_McCoy" title="Matthew Edmond McCoy">Matthew Edmond McCoy</a> (1868–1929), served as Magistrate of Pitcairn Island in 1909</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gerard_Bromley_Robert_Christian" title="Gerard Bromley Robert Christian">Gerard Bromley Robert Christian</a> (1870–1919), served as Magistrate of Pitcairn Island from 1910 to 1919</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Edgar_Allen_Christian" title="Edgar Allen Christian">Edgar Allen Christian</a> (1879–1960), a politician from Pitcairn and Chief Magistrate of Pitcairn Island on several occasions between 1923 and 1939</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Charles_Richard_Parkin_Christian" title="Charles Richard Parkin Christian">Charles Richard Parkin Christian</a> (1883–1971), a long-serving politician from Pitcairn and Chief Magistrate of Pitcairn Island for eleven years at various times between 1920 and 1957</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Frederick_Martin_Christian" title="Frederick Martin Christian">Frederick Martin Christian</a> (1883–1971), a politician from Pitcairn and Chief Magistrate of Pitcairn Island on three occasions between 1921 and 1943</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Lorenzo_Christian" title="John Lorenzo Christian">John Lorenzo Christian</a> (1895–1984), twice served as Chief Magistrate of Pitcairn Island: 1952–54 and 1961–66</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ivan_Christian" title="Ivan Christian">Ivan Christian</a> (1919–1991), a politician from Pitcairn and Chief Magistrate of Pitcairn Island from 1976 to 1984</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tom_Christian" title="Tom Christian">Tom Christian</a> (1935–2013), radio operator</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Brenda_Christian" title="Brenda Christian">Brenda Christian</a> (born 1953), a political figure from the Pitcairn Islands and served the territory as its first female Mayor from 8 November to 15 December 2004</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jay_Warren" title="Jay Warren">Jay Warren</a> (born 1956), a political figure who served as the 3rd Mayor of Pitcairn Islands</li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Further_reading">Further reading</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Further reading">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mutiny_on_the_Bounty"><i>Mutiny on the </i>Bounty<i> </i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Mutiny on the Bounty">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<li><i>The Discovery of Fletcher Christian: A Travel Book</i> by Glynn Christian, a descendant of <a href="/wiki/Fletcher_Christian" title="Fletcher Christian">Fletcher Christian, Bounty Mutineer</a> (Guild Press, London, 2005 pp.&#160;448)</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="After_the_Mutiny">After the Mutiny</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pitcairn_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: After the Mutiny">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<li><i><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://doi.org/10.26686/vuwlr.v38i3.5533">The "Re-colonising of Pitcairn</a></i> by Sue Farran, Senior Lecturer, University of Dundee; Visiting Lecturer, University of the South Pacific.</li>
<li>Ball, Ian M. – <i>Pitcairn: Children of Mutiny.</i> 1973</li>
<li>Belcher, Lady – <i>The Mutineers of the Bounty and Their Descendants in Pitcairn and Norfolk Islands.</i> 1870</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dea_Birkett" title="Dea Birkett">Birkett, Dea</a> – <i>Serpent in Paradise.</i> Anchor Doubleday, 1997. <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/0-385-48870-X" title="Special:BookSources/0-385-48870-X">0-385-48870-X</a>.</li>

<li>Christian, Glynn – <i>Fragile Paradise: The Discovery of Fletcher Christian, Bounty Mutineer.</i> 2005</li>
<li>Clarke, Peter – <i>Hell and Paradise: The Norfolk-Bounty-Pitcairn Saga.</i> 1986</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/William_Young_Fullerton" title="William Young Fullerton">Fullerton, W. Y.</a> – <i>The Romance of Pitcairn Island.</i> 1923</li>
<li>Hancock, W. K. – <i>Politics in Pitcairn and Other Essays.</i> 1947</li>
<li>Lucas, Charles – <i>The Pitcairn Island Register Book.</i> 1929</li>

<li>Oliver, Douglas – <i>Return to Tahiti: Bligh's Second Breadfruit Voyage.</i> 1988</li>
<li>Randall, John E. – <i>Reef and Shore Fishes of the South Pacific: New Caledonia to Tahiti and the Pitcairn Islands.</i> 2005</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Harry_L._Shapiro" title="Harry L. Shapiro">Shapiro, Harry L.</a> – <i>The Heritage of the 'Bounty': The Story of Pitcairn Through Six Generations.</i> 1936</li>
<li>Silverman, David – <i>Pitcairn Island.</i> 1967</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/George_Tobin_(Royal_Navy_officer)" title="George Tobin (Royal Navy officer)">Tobin, George, Lt.</a> – <i>Captain Bligh's Second Chance: An eyewitness account of his return to the South Seas.</i> 2007</li></ul>
